ID: scrubber-main-lineup-selection
Title: How to choose between PRED750, H2KM, BD2K, PAS2400, and PAS5000
Product: Scrubber Selector
Category: Product Selection
Source: MonSuite Scrubber Selector | /scrubber-selector
Keywords: scrubber selector, PRED750, H2KM, BD2K, PAS2400, PAS5000, which scrubber, residential, wet, metal, plastic
Questions:
- Which scrubber should I choose?
- What are the main scrubbers?
- H2KM vs BD2K vs PAS2400
- Which scrubber for residential?
- Which scrubber for large containment?
Answer:
Use PRED750 for smaller/portable 750 CFM-class jobs. Use H2KM/H2KMA as the main metal 2,000 CFM workhorse for most commercial/industrial/heavy containment jobs. Use BD2K-XHP/XHPA when the same 2,000 CFM-class work benefits from a plastic cabinet, especially finished residential areas, scratch-sensitive spaces, or wet/damp restoration work. Use PAS2400 when 2,100 CFM and 120V power are needed with better mobility/stair handling. Use PAS5000 for very large containments where 4,000 CFM is useful and 230V/30A power is available.

ID: scrubber-pas5000-overview
Title: PAS5000 4,000 CFM large scrubber overview
Product: PAS5000
Category: Scrubber Selector
Source: PAS5000 Instruction Manual | /scrubber-selector
Keywords: PAS5000, 4000 CFM, 230 VAC, 30 amp, 21 amps, large containment, galvanized steel, H242406-99, VL2024, H2080P
Questions:
- What is the PAS5000?
- How much airflow does PAS5000 have?
- What power does PAS5000 need?
- When should I use PAS5000?
Answer:
PAS5000 is the large real Abatement HEPA-AIRE portable air filtration unit for major containments. It is rated at 4,000 CFM with clean filters and requires 230 VAC, 60 Hz, single phase, 30A power; normal operation draws up to about 21A. It uses two H242406-99 HEPA filters, two H2002 pleated filters, optional H2001 polypad pre-filters, and optional VL2024 carbon filters. Use it only when the job has the access, power, and scale to justify a 332 lb high-airflow unit.

ID: scrubber-pas2400-overview
Title: PAS2400 2,100 CFM portable air scrubber overview
Product: PAS2400
Category: Scrubber Selector
Source: PAS2400 Instruction Manual | /scrubber-selector
Keywords: PAS2400, 2100 CFM, 700 CFM, 120 VAC, 15 amp, 12 amps, stair climber, stainless steel, H1980P, VL2002
Questions:
- What is the PAS2400?
- How much airflow does PAS2400 have?
- What power does PAS2400 need?
- Is PAS2400 good for stairs?
Answer:
PAS2400 is a real Abatement HEPA-AIRE portable air scrubber with 700 CFM low speed and 2,100 CFM high speed clean-filter airflow. It runs on 120 VAC/60 Hz with a minimum 15A circuit and draws 12A or less. It has a stainless steel two-module design, transport dolly, stair climber assembly, and an included H1980P 12-inch manifold that can attach to intake or exhaust. Use it when higher airflow is needed on 120V power and mobility/stairs matter.

ID: scrubber-bd2k-overview
Title: BD2K-XHP / BD2K-XHPA plastic scrubber overview
Product: BD2K-XHP / BD2K-XHPA
Category: Scrubber Selector
Source: BD2K-XHP/BD2K-XHPA Instruction Manual | /scrubber-selector

Questions:
- What is BD2K?
- When should I choose BD2K?
- Is BD2K better for residential?
- Is BD2K plastic?
- BD2K vs H2KM
Answer:
BD2K-XHP/BD2K-XHPA is the plastic-cabinet BULLDOG portable air filtration unit in the same general class as H2KM. MonSuite treats it as the preferred 2,000 CFM-class option for residential/finished spaces where metal could scratch surfaces, and for wet/damp restoration areas where plastic is preferred. Use H2KM metal everywhere else unless the job specifically benefits from the BD2K plastic cabinet.

ID: scrubber-h2km-h2kma-overview
Title: H2KM / H2KMA 2,000 CFM metal scrubber overview
Product: H2KM / H2KMA
Category: Scrubber Selector
Source: H2KM/H2KMA Instruction Manual | /scrubber-selector
Keywords: H2KM, H2KMA, 2000 CFM, 1300 CFM, metal cabinet, galvanized steel, H2001, H2002, H242406-99, VL2024
Questions:
- What is the H2KM?
- What is the H2KMA?
- How much airflow does H2KM have?
- When should I choose H2KM instead of BD2K?
Answer:
H2KM/H2KMA is a real Abatement HEPA-AIRE portable air filtration unit in the 2,000 CFM class. It is the metal-cabinet workhorse: 1,300 CFM low speed and 2,000 CFM high speed with clean filters, 120 VAC/15A power, 10A or less normal operating amps, and 20 ga galvanized steel cabinet construction. Use H2KM/H2KMA for most commercial, industrial, healthcare construction, abatement, and general containment jobs where a metal cabinet is appropriate. For finished residential spaces, scratch-sensitive areas, or wet/damp restoration work, choose BD2K plastic instead.

ID: scrubber-pred750-electrical-ducting
Title: PRED750 electrical and ducting notes
Product: PRED750
Category: Setup
Source: PRED750 Instruction Manual | /scrubber-selector
Keywords: PRED750, 120 VAC, 15 amp, 1.9 amps, 10 inch duct, ducting, GFCI, stackable
Questions:
- What power does PRED750 need?
- What duct size is PRED750?
- How many amps does PRED750 draw?
- Can PRED750 be ducted?
Answer:
PRED750 is listed in MonSuite with 120 VAC, 60 Hz, 15A circuit requirements, normal operating amps of 1.9A or less, and integrated 10-inch nominal inlet and outlet connections. Use the shortest ducting possible, minimize bends, prefer rigid duct where practical, and keep flex duct taut because ducting and restrictions reduce airflow.

ID: scrubber-pred750-filters
Title: PRED750 filters and carbon option
Product: PRED750
Category: Filters
Source: PRED750 Instruction Manual | /scrubber-selector
Keywords: PRED750, F621, H502, H161606-99, VL1002, VAPOR-LOCK, carbon, HEPA, VOC, odor
Questions:
- What filters does the PRED750 use?
- Does PRED750 have carbon filter option?
- Can PRED750 remove odors?
- What is the HEPA part number for PRED750?
Answer:
PRED750 supplied filter stages are F621 1-inch coarse pre-filter, H502 2-inch pleated pre-filter, and H161606-99 HEPA filter. For low concentrations of odors, vapors, gases, or VOC support, use the optional VL1002 VAPOR-LOCK carbon filter. HEPA captures particles; carbon is the option for odor/VOC support.

It uses staged pre-filtration plus HEPA filtration, has a variable-speed clean-filter airflow range of 200 to 750 CFM, and can be configured for negative pressure, recirculation, or positive pressure support when ducting and site setup are appropriate. ID: pressure-guide-pressure-failure-causes
Title: what causes pressure failure
Product: Pressure Guide
Category: Troubleshooting
Source: Pressure Monitoring Guide | /pressure-monitoring
Keywords: pressure failure, alarm, doors, barriers, filters, hvac, tubing, ducts
Questions:
- Why did pressure fail?
- Why is my room not holding pressure?
- Why is the pressure alarm going off?
- What should I check when pressure is unstable?
Answer:
Common pressure failure causes include doors left open, bad seals, torn barriers, clogged filters, undersized HEPA units, failed fans, blocked ducts, too much entry/exit traffic, HVAC imbalance, incorrect tubing setup, wind, or stack effect. Continuous monitoring matters because a manual reading only shows one moment.

ID: pressure-guide-hepa-vs-merv
Title: HEPA versus MERV filtration
Product: Pressure Guide
Category: Filtration
Source: Pressure Monitoring Guide | /pressure-monitoring
Keywords: hepa, merv, filter, filtration, air scrubber, hvac
Questions:
- What is the difference between HEPA and MERV?
- Does MERV mean HEPA?
- What filtration should I use for containment?
Answer:
MERV ratings usually apply to building HVAC filters. HEPA is higher-efficiency filtration used for containment, isolation support, remediation, negative air machines, and portable air cleaning. A HEPA unit moves and filters air; a pressure monitor verifies whether the space is holding the correct pressure relationship.

ID: pressure-guide-ach-math
Title: basic ACH math
Product: Pressure Guide
Category: ACH Calculator
Source: Pressure Monitoring Guide | /pressure-monitoring
Keywords: ach formula, cfm formula, room volume, calculate ach, calculate cfm
Questions:
- How do I calculate ACH?
- How do I calculate CFM needed?
- What is the ACH formula?
- How much CFM do I need for 12 ACH?
Answer:
Room volume is length × width × height. ACH = (CFM × 60) / room volume. CFM needed = (target ACH × room volume) / 60. This estimates airflow only and does not prove pressure, containment, or compliance.

ID: pressure-guide-ach-vs-pressure
Title: ACH versus pressure
Product: Pressure Guide
Category: ACH
Source: Pressure Monitoring Guide | /pressure-monitoring
Keywords: ach, air changes, cfm, pressure, ventilation, dilution
Questions:
- What is ACH?
- Is ACH the same as pressure?
- Can a room have ACH but fail pressure?
- Can negative pressure have poor ventilation?
Answer:
ACH means Air Changes per Hour. It describes how many times the air in a room is replaced or cleaned in one hour. ACH is about dilution and air cleaning. Pressure is about air direction. They are related, but they are not the same thing.

ID: pressure-guide-common-pressure-targets
Title: common pressure targets
Product: Pressure Guide
Category: Pressure Targets
Source: Pressure Monitoring Guide | /pressure-monitoring
Keywords: pressure targets, pa, in w.g., -2.5 pa, -7.5 pa, -0.01, -0.02, -0.03
Questions:
- What pressure target should I use?
- What is a common isolation room pressure?
- What is a common construction containment pressure?
- What is -0.01 in water?
Answer:
Common reference points include US airborne isolation around -2.5 Pa / -0.01 in. w.g., Canadian airborne isolation around -7.5 Pa / -0.03 in. w.g., and construction or abatement containment around -5 Pa / -0.02 in. w.g. Always confirm the actual target with the facility policy, project specification, consultant, and AHJ.

ID: pressure-guide-positive-pressure
Title: positive pressure explanation
Product: Pressure Guide
Category: Positive Pressure
Source: Pressure Monitoring Guide | /pressure-monitoring
Keywords: positive pressure, operating room, sterile storage, protective environment, clean space
Questions:
- What is positive pressure?
- Why use positive pressure?
- What spaces need positive pressure?
- How does positive pressure protect a clean room?
Answer:
Positive pressure means air is being pushed out of the protected space. It is used when the space itself needs protection from outside contamination, such as operating rooms, sterile storage, protective environments, clean rooms, pharmacies, and sensitive clinical spaces.

ID: pressure-guide-negative-pressure
Title: negative pressure explanation
Product: Pressure Guide
Category: Negative Pressure
Source: Pressure Monitoring Guide | /pressure-monitoring
Keywords: negative pressure, isolation, containment, infection, dust, asbestos, mold, silica
Questions:
- What is negative pressure?
- Why use negative pressure?
- How does negative pressure protect a hallway?
- What rooms need negative pressure?
Answer:
Negative pressure means air is being pulled into a room or containment area. It is used when air inside the space may be dirty, infectious, dusty, or hazardous. The goal is to keep contaminated air from escaping into surrounding clean or occupied areas.

ID: pressure-guide-pressure-monitoring-purpose
Title: what pressure monitors do
Product: Pressure Guide
Category: Pressure Monitoring
Source: Pressure Monitoring Guide | /pressure-monitoring
Keywords: pressure, monitoring, negative pressure, positive pressure, air direction, containment
Questions:
- What does a pressure monitor do?
- Does a pressure monitor clean air?
- Does pressure monitoring prove ACH?
- What is the purpose of pressure monitoring?
Answer:
Pressure monitors confirm whether air is moving in the intended direction between two spaces. They show whether a room or containment is negative or positive compared with a reference area. They do not clean air, filter air, or prove ACH by themselves.

Answer:
Make a support ticket when an issue affects a live hospital job, pressure readings look wrong, pressure alarms will not clear, cloud/mobile verification fails, recording/history is missing, or the documentation does not clearly answer the question. Include product, serial number if available, firmware version, current pressure reading, alarm points, job/site name, screenshots/photos, and troubleshooting already tried.

ID: pressure-calibration-zero-pressure
Title: zero/calibrate pressure reading safely
Product: RPM / PPM4 / Pressure Sensor
Category: Calibration
Source: RPM / PPM4 calibration guidance | https://docs.google.com/document/d/1fQubA6975M3K3Zy80_ylF3fbROo8CGRp/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=106548179376433005372&rtpof=true&sd=true
Keywords: pressure, calibrate, calibration, zero, zero pressure, offset, sensor, do not blow, ports
Questions:
- How do I calibrate pressure?
- How do I zero pressure?
- Why is pressure not zero?
- How do I reset pressure reading?
- Can I blow into the pressure port?
Answer:
When zeroing or calibrating pressure, remove applied pressure from the inputs and follow the monitor calibration workflow. Do not blow into hoses or pressure ports. If the reading will not settle, check tubing, filters, reference location, and whether the sensor is exposed to actual pressure before assuming the monitor is wrong.

ID: pressure-tubing-room-reference-check
Title: check Room and Reference pressure tubing
Product: RPM / PPM4 / Pressure Sensor
Category: Tubing
Source: Pressure Sensor Quick Guide | https://docs.google.com/document/d/1cOVjqPVg3SVflLI92JfK1A26HadMA2U1/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=106548179376433005372&rtpof=true&sd=true
Keywords: pressure, tubing, hose, room, reference, ref, ports, differential pressure, negative pressure, sensor damage
Questions:
- How do I connect pressure tubing?
- What are Room and Reference ports?
- Why is pressure reading wrong?
- How do I check pressure hoses?
- Can tubing be reversed?
Answer:
Pressure readings depend on the Room and Reference tubing being connected correctly. Verify the Room line goes to the monitored space and the Reference line goes to the intended reference area. Check for reversed hoses, kinks, disconnections, blocked filters, or leaks. Do not blow into hoses or pressure ports because that can damage the sensor.

Do not remove the USB drive until the download is complete. Downloading history does not necessarily erase internal records unless Delete Log is used.

ID: pressure-maintain-required-pressure
Title: maintain required pressure
Product: RPM / PPM4
Category: Pressure Control
Source: Pressure monitoring best-practice guidance | https://docs.google.com/document/d/1cOVjqPVg3SVflLI92JfK1A26HadMA2U1/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=106548179376433005372&rtpof=true&sd=true
Keywords: maintain pressure, keep pressure, negative pressure, positive pressure, stable pressure, containment, fan, airflow, leak, door, filter
Questions:
- How do I maintain pressure?
- How do I keep negative pressure?
- How do I keep pressure stable?
- What if pressure keeps dropping?
- How do I hold pressure in a room?
- How do I maintain the required pressure?
Answer:
To maintain required pressure, treat the monitor as the proof device and fix the room conditions causing the reading. Verify tubing and reference location, keep doors and containment openings controlled, check for leaks, confirm exhaust/negative-air equipment is running correctly, make sure filters are not clogged, and adjust airflow or containment before changing alarm limits.

ID: pressure-alarms-active-pressure-alarm-troubleshooting
Title: active pressure alarm troubleshooting
Product: RPM / PPM4
Category: Pressure Alarms
Source: RPM / PPM4 pressure alarm guidance | https://docs.google.com/document/d/1fQubA6975M3K3Zy80_ylF3fbROo8CGRp/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=106548179376433005372&rtpof=true&sd=true
Keywords: pressure, alarm, active alarm, red screen, beeping, outside limit, threshold, troubleshooting, negative pressure
Questions:
- Why is my pressure alarm going off?
- Why is the screen red?
- Why is the monitor alarming?
- What do I check when pressure alarm is active?
- How do I troubleshoot pressure alarms?
Answer:
If a pressure alarm is active, first check whether the live pressure reading is outside the selected alarm limits. Then check Room/Reference tubing, reversed or disconnected hoses, blocked filters, doors/openings, containment leaks, fan or negative-air-machine changes, and whether the alarm points were set correctly. STOP ALARM silences the audible alert only; it does not fix the pressure condition.

ID: ppm4-rpm-power-ppm4-and-rpm-expansion-bus-capacity

Product: PPM4 / RPM
Category: Power
Source: PPM4 / RPM |

Answer:
PPM4 and RPM-S expansion bus capacity is modeled as 200mA. Both have two Ethernet ports. Sensors can daisy-chain, but the total load on a monitor-powered chain should stay at or below 200mA unless a Power Bus or local power accessory is used.

ID: ppm4-rpm-power-what-happens-if-bus-power-is-exceeded
Title: what happens if bus power is exceeded
Product: PPM4 / RPM
Category: Power
Source: PPM4 / RPM | 
Keywords: bus, charger, exceeded, happens, ma, power, power bus, ppm4, rpm, warning, what
Questions:
- what happens if bus power is exceeded?
- How much power for PPM4 / RPM?
- Why am I getting a lightning warning?
- What does the power warning mean for PPM4 / RPM?
- How do I fix power issue on PPM4 / RPM?
- How do I what happens if bus power is exceeded?
- How to what happens if bus power is exceeded?
- What do I need to know about what happens if bus power is exceeded?
- Help with what happens if bus power is exceeded?
- PPM4 / RPM what happens if bus power is exceeded?
Answer:
If the daisy chain exceeds the 200mA bus limit, the whole chain should be considered unsupported or unreliable. Do not assume only the last sensor fails. Possible symptoms include voltage drop, intermittent sensor detection, unstable readings, or devices randomly disconnecting.

ID: ppm4-rpm-power-fix-an-overloaded-sensor-chain
Title: fix an overloaded sensor chain
Product: PPM4 / RPM
Category: Power
Source: PPM4 / RPM | 
Keywords: bus, chain, charger, fix, ma, overloaded, power, power bus, ppm4, rpm, sensor, warning
Questions:
- fix an overloaded sensor chain?
- How much power for PPM4 / RPM?
- Why am I getting a lightning warning?
- What does the power warning mean for PPM4 / RPM?
- How do I fix power issue on PPM4 / RPM?
- How do I fix an overloaded sensor chain?
- How to fix an overloaded sensor chain?
- What do I need to know about fix an overloaded sensor chain?
- Help with fix an overloaded sensor chain?
- PPM4 / RPM fix an overloaded sensor chain?
Answer:
To fix an overloaded sensor chain, add a Power Bus, split the chain, or attach a PPM4 Charger to high-draw devices. Example: two Temp/RH sensors and one Pressure sensor draw 165mA. Adding ACH adds 100mA for 265mA total, which exceeds 200mA. If the ACH has local power, the bus load returns to 165mA.

PPM4CHRGR is modeled as a local single-device power accessory. It powers only the one device it is attached to. If attached to an ACH sensor, that ACH sensor stays in the Ethernet communication chain but no longer counts against upstream bus power load.

Category: Battery

Before first use, the PPM4 battery should be conditioned. Turn the power on and let it operate in AC mode until the battery discharges completely. Then charge until full charge is achieved. Use only the Abatement-supplied charger. Keep the battery charged every 6 months even if unused.

ID: ppm4-operation-ppm4-ac-mode-versus-battery-mode
Title: PPM4 AC mode versus battery mode
Product: PPM4
Category: Operation
Source: PPM4 Manual | https://docs.google.com/document/d/16K9WVfojveqaZWyiTfti14W0QO2rAuhv/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=106548179376433005372&rtpof=true&sd=true
Keywords: ac, battery, manual, mode, operation, ppm4, versus
Questions:
- PPM4 AC mode versus battery mode?
- How do I ppm4 ac mode versus battery mode?
- PPM4 help PPM4 AC mode versus battery mode?
- Where is PPM4 AC mode versus battery mode in the manual?
- PPM4 Operation question?
- How to ppm4 ac mode versus battery mode?
- What do I need to know about ppm4 ac mode versus battery mode?
- Help with ppm4 ac mode versus battery mode?
- PPM4 PPM4 AC mode versus battery mode?
Answer:
PPM4 has AC Power Mode for continuous operation and Battery Mode for preserving battery life. In AC mode the monitor is active and the display times out while the LED stays on. In Battery Mode the display switches off after inactivity and the LED flashes periodically to show the unit remains operating.

ID: rpm-sensors-rpm-sensor-capacity
Title: RPM sensor capacity
Product: RPM
Category: Sensors
Source: RPM Manual | https://docs.google.com/document/d/1fQubA6975M3K3Zy80_ylF3fbROo8CGRp/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=106548179376433005372&rtpof=true&sd=true

- RPM RPM sensor capacity?
Answer:
RPM supports connection of up to 10 external sensors, with a maximum of 5 per room. The built-in pressure sensor is automatically assigned to Room 1, Sensor 1, so that address cannot be used by an external sensor.

ID: rpm-connections-rpm-do-not-connect-to-standard-network
Title: RPM do not connect to standard network
Product: RPM
Category: Connections
Source: RPM Manual | https://docs.google.com/document/d/1fQubA6975M3K3Zy80_ylF3fbROo8CGRp/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=106548179376433005372&rtpof=true&sd=true

- Where is RPM do not connect to standard network in the manual?
- RPM Connections question?
- How to rpm do not connect to standard network?
- What do I need to know about rpm do not connect to standard network?
- Help with rpm do not connect to standard network?
- RPM RPM do not connect to standard network?
Answer:
Important: do not connect any RPM or sensor unit to a standard Ethernet network switch or router. The RJ45 connections are for the sensor bus, not normal Ethernet networking, and improper connection may damage RPM, sensors, or network equipment.

Product: RPM
Category: Sensors
Source: RPM Manual | https://docs.google.com/document/d/1fQubA6975M3K3Zy80_ylF3fbROo8CGRp/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=106548179376433005372&rtpof=true&sd=true

- How to rpm sensor setup dip switches?
- What do I need to know about rpm sensor setup dip switches?
- Help with rpm sensor setup dip switches?
- RPM RPM sensor setup DIP switches?
Answer:
Before connecting RPM sensors, plan the sensor layout. Each external sensor must be assigned a room and sensor number using DIP switches. Room 1 Sensor 1 is reserved for the built-in pressure sensor and cannot be assigned to an external sensor.

ID: rpm-connections-rpm-connect-sensors
Title: RPM connect sensors
Product: RPM
Category: Connections
Source: RPM Manual | https://docs.google.com/document/d/1fQubA6975M3K3Zy80_ylF3fbROo8CGRp/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=106548179376433005372&rtpof=true&sd=true
Keywords: connect, connections, manual, rpm, sensors
Questions:
- RPM connect sensors?
- How do I rpm connect sensors?
- RPM help RPM connect sensors?
- Where is RPM connect sensors in the manual?
- RPM Connections question?
- How to rpm connect sensors?
- What do I need to know about rpm connect sensors?
- Help with rpm connect sensors?
- RPM RPM connect sensors?
Answer:
Use straight-through Cat5e or higher Ethernet cable for RPM sensor connections. Connect from either RJ45 port on the RPM to either RJ45 port on the sensor. Multiple sensors can be daisy-chained sensor-to-sensor. Maximum total cable distance from RPM to the last sensor should not exceed 50 ft.

Source: ACH / Velocity Sensor Quick Guide | https://docs.google.com/document/d/1of_UXpkELLnhNzrOpBh5U6la_a6zu--D/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=106548179376433005372&rtpof=true&sd=true
Keywords: ach, guide, placement, quick, sensor, velocity
Questions:
- ACH sensor placement?
- How do I ach sensor placement?
- ACH / Velocity Sensor help ACH sensor placement?
- Where is ACH sensor placement in the manual?
- ACH / Velocity Sensor Placement question?
- How to ach sensor placement?
- What do I need to know about ach sensor placement?
- Help with ach sensor placement?
- ACH / Velocity Sensor ACH sensor placement?
Answer:
Install the ACH/Velocity sensor where airflow needs to be monitored. Position the sensing element or probe so airflow can reach it. Avoid blocked areas, tight corners, water spray, heavy dust, debris, physical damage, or supply/exhaust points unless those are the intended measurement locations.

Answer:
MonSuite models the ACH/Velocity sensor with a remote probe that must stay within 2 ft of the ACH sensor. This rule is intended for future ducting layouts and should be confirmed against final engineering documentation.

This manual will outline the operation and functionality of the Abatement Technologies Portable Pressure Monitor PPM4 in detail. Visual aids have been added for additional help in proper setup and installation as well as navigating the system’s interface. Rugged case suitable for harsh environments. Optionally operate with the case open or closed or with optional hanging bracket. USB port for on-site firmware upgrading and archive history downloading as well as optional web and mobile app connection for offsite monitoring Differential pressure measurement in the ranges of -25Pa (-0.1 inWC) to +25Pa (+0.1 inWC).

ID: ppm4-document-reference-ppm4-manual-reference-chunk-2-note-that-this-i
Title: PPM4 Manual reference chunk 2: Note that this instrument is operated from an internal
Product: PPM4
Category: Document Reference
Source: PPM4 Manual | https://docs.google.com/document/d/16K9WVfojveqaZWyiTfti14W0QO2rAuhv/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=106548179376433005372&rtpof=true&sd=true
Keywords: abatement, achieved, another, approximately, battery, before, charge, charged, charger, chunk, completely, conditioned, damage, depleted, discharges, document, even, every, from, full, fully, hours, instrument, internal, manual, note, operated, ppm4, reference, that, this
Questions:
- What does the PPM4 Manual say about Note that this instrument is operated from an internal?
- Find PPM4 information about Note that this instrument is operated from an internal?
- Search PPM4 Manual for Note that this instrument is operated from an internal?
- I need help with PPM4: Note that this instrument is operated from an internal?
- Where in the docs is Note that this instrument is operated from an internal?
- PPM4 Manual reference chunk 2: Note that this instrument is operated from an internal?
- How do I ppm4 manual reference chunk 2: note that this instrument is operated from an internal?
- How to ppm4 manual reference chunk 2: note that this instrument is operated from an internal?
- What do I need to know about ppm4 manual reference chunk 2: note that this instrument is operated from an internal?
- Help with ppm4 manual reference chunk 2: note that this instrument is operated from an internal?
- PPM4 PPM4 Manual reference chunk 2: Note that this instrument is operated from an internal?
Answer:
Note that this instrument is operated from an internal Lithium Polymer battery. This is a rechargeable battery which must be conditioned before use. When receiving the unit, turn the power on using the ON/OFF switch and leave it to operate in AC mode until the battery discharges completely. The screen and the LED will go off when the battery is depleted. The unit should then be charged until a full charge is achieved (approximately 7 hours). Note: Only use the Abatement supplied battery charger (MSS235). Use of another power supply can damage the unit. The unit is now ready for use. Please note that the battery should be fully charged every 6 months even if the instrument is not used.

ID: ppm4-document-reference-ppm4-manual-reference-chunk-3-the-ppm4-pressur
Title: PPM4 Manual reference chunk 3: The PPM4 pressure monitor operates in two different modes
Product: PPM4
Category: Document Reference
Source: PPM4 Manual | https://docs.google.com/document/d/16K9WVfojveqaZWyiTfti14W0QO2rAuhv/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=106548179376433005372&rtpof=true&sd=true
Keywords: able, active, after, always, approximately, automatically, battery, charger, chosen, chunk, continuous, continuously, designed, different, differing, display, document, from, hours, inactivity, indicator, installed, manual, modes, monitor, operates, ppm4, pressure, reference, the, two
Questions:
- What does the PPM4 Manual say about The PPM4 pressure monitor operates in two different modes?
- Find PPM4 information about The PPM4 pressure monitor operates in two different modes?
- Search PPM4 Manual for The PPM4 pressure monitor operates in two different modes?
- I need help with PPM4: The PPM4 pressure monitor operates in two different modes?
- Where in the docs is The PPM4 pressure monitor operates in two different modes?
- PPM4 Manual reference chunk 3: The PPM4 pressure monitor operates in two different modes?
- How do I ppm4 manual reference chunk 3: the ppm4 pressure monitor operates in two different modes?
- How to ppm4 manual reference chunk 3: the ppm4 pressure monitor operates in two different modes?
- What do I need to know about ppm4 manual reference chunk 3: the ppm4 pressure monitor operates in two different modes?
- Help with ppm4 manual reference chunk 3: the ppm4 pressure monitor operates in two different modes?
- PPM4 PPM4 Manual reference chunk 3: The PPM4 pressure monitor operates in two different modes?
Answer:
The PPM4 pressure monitor operates in two different modes to satisfy differing requirements from the operator. These modes are the Battery Mode and the AC Power Mode. The AC Power Mode is designed for continuous operation when the operator wishes to be able to view the display at all times. In this mode, the display screen is always active and the LED indicator is ON continuously. This mode is optimally used with the Battery Charger installed and powered. If this Battery Mode is chosen with the on-screen menu and the unit is operated only from the battery, battery life is limited to approximately 8 hours. The Battery Mode is designed to preserve battery life for unattended operation. In this mode the display is automatically switched off after one minute of user inactivity. In this mode, the LED on the front of the unit will flash every 5 seconds to indicate that the unit is still operating and has not been turned off. The display will only re-appear in the event of an alarm event or if the screen is touched by the user. After touching the screen or pressing the power button, the display will appear but will switch off again after one minute. In Battery Mode, the unit will monitor pressures for up to 3days from a fully charged battery. Both modes are explained in detail later in this document. Note that pressure monitoring and Alarm Annunciation is identical in both modes. In addition, all events are saved in the internal archive in exactly the same manner for both modes. The difference between the modes is only in the appearance of the display.

ID: ppm4-document-reference-ppm4-manual-reference-chunk-4-the-ac-or-batter
Title: PPM4 Manual reference chunk 4: The AC or Battery modes are selected through the
Product: PPM4
Category: Document Reference
Source: PPM4 Manual | https://docs.google.com/document/d/16K9WVfojveqaZWyiTfti14W0QO2rAuhv/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=106548179376433005372&rtpof=true&sd=true
Keywords: administration, are, attached, battery, behavior, button, case, chunk, connected, document, finally, first, holding, hose, icon, input, knows, labeled, life, lost, manual, maximized, menu, mode, modes, ppm4, reference, selected, the, through
Questions:
- What does the PPM4 Manual say about The AC or Battery modes are selected through the?
- Find PPM4 information about The AC or Battery modes are selected through the?
- Search PPM4 Manual for The AC or Battery modes are selected through the?
- I need help with PPM4: The AC or Battery modes are selected through the?
- Where in the docs is The AC or Battery modes are selected through the?
- PPM4 Manual reference chunk 4: The AC or Battery modes are selected through the?
- How do I ppm4 manual reference chunk 4: the ac or battery modes are selected through the?
- How to ppm4 manual reference chunk 4: the ac or battery modes are selected through the?
- What do I need to know about ppm4 manual reference chunk 4: the ac or battery modes are selected through the?
- Help with ppm4 manual reference chunk 4: the ac or battery modes are selected through the?
- PPM4 PPM4 Manual reference chunk 4: The AC or Battery modes are selected through the?
Answer:
The AC or Battery modes are selected through the administration menu. Note that, if the unit is set in Battery Mode and the power supply is attached, the icon will still show as Battery Mode. In this case, the TFT screen stays ON at all times since the unit knows that it has a source of power. If power is lost, the screen will revert to its normal Battery Mode behavior. Finally, when the unit is turned off by holding the ON/OFF button, the LED will remain off at all times. In this mode, battery life is maximized when it is not necessary for the unit to record and monitor pressures. There are two pressure sensor input ports on the pressure monitor. The first input port, labeled REF, is used as a reference pressure. The other end of the hose connected to this port must be in an area outside the space being monitored. If the pressure monitor is located outside the room being monitored, no hose needs to be connected to the REFERENCE input. The other end of the hose from the room being monitored is connected to the input port labeled ROOM.

Answer:
A USB connector is provided for downloading the history file and for uploading new versions of firmware. The USB connector is designed for USB flash drives only. This switch is used to activate the display by a short press of the button. To switch the main power ON and OFF the ON/OFF button will need to be held for approx. 3 seconds. The mode can be observed by viewing the indicator LED on the front of the unit. The LED is on when power is ON. This power jack is used to connect the battery charger to the unit. While the charger is connected, battery charging is automatic. The unit will operate indefinitely with the charger connected. - Help with ppm4 manual reference chunk 6: to operate the unit after initial charging, connect the?
- PPM4 PPM4 Manual reference chunk 6: To operate the unit after initial charging, connect the?
Answer:
To operate the unit after initial charging, connect the 1/8” tubing to the ROOM and REF input ports using the supplied quick disconnects. The quick disconnects are supplied with a right-angled adaptor to allow operation with the cover closed. Slots are provided in the base of the case to allow the tubing, the power supply cable and the BMS cable to pass to the outside when closed lid operation is desired. To be able to keep the battery charged while operating, the Battery Charger cable must be inserted into the power jack. If the system is operating in Battery Mode, be advised that the display will turn off after 1 minute of inactivity (Battery Mode is explained later in this document). Operating in AC Power Mode will keep the display on at all times. The filters provided with the box should be placed in line with each input tube in a convenient location. The arrow on the filter must point towards the HHPM on both tubes. NOTE: Caution must be exercised when closing the lid to ensure that the tubes and cables are not pinched. They must pass through their allotted slots.

Keywords: access, accessed, alarm, alarms, appear, audible, back, bottom, button, buttons, chunk, comprised, condition, connected, corner, document, external, five, gear, icon, main, manual, may, menu, monitor, ppm4, pressure, reference, settings, the, using

Answer:
The pressure monitor settings may be accessed using the menu system. The menu system is comprised of a main menu screen and several sub screens. The main menu (shown on the right) is accessed by pushing the settings (gear) icon in the upper right corner of the main screen. The title of the menu is shown at the top. There are five submenu buttons in this menu screen which can be pushed to access the respective submenu. The BACK button is used for navigating to the previous menu. In an alarm condition, the STOP ALARM button will appear at the bottom of the main menu screen. Pressing the STOP ALARM button will silence the audible alarm as well as any external alarms through the web or mobile app if connected. The main screen will remain red until the differential pressure is within the user specified limit. The EXIT button is used for returning to the main screen. A detailed description of the menu structure and the available settings are outlined later in this document. The Room Settings menu will allow you to change various settings for the monitored room.

Set or change the room name. The room name appears on the Main Screen above the sensor readings. If you click this name, a menu will appear to change this name. The Sensor Settings sub menu allows the user to enable/disable sensors, enable/disable alarms, and set the alarm point values. Alarm point values sets the alarm limit of the acceptable pressure range. If the room type is negative, anything above this limit will cause the system to alarm. If the room type is positive anything below this limit will cause the system to alarm. Range: -9.960 Pa (-0.0400 inWC) ~ 9.960 Pa (0.0400 inWC). Factory default is set to 0.0000 inWC. The history archive menu allows the operator to interact with the log history. The history will display the job number which was set when the event was recorded. Offloading the historical information from the PPM4 does not destroy any information stored internally. The download process will start and the operator will be returned to the previous menu when the download is complete. Do not remove the USB device until the download is complete. The history is downloaded in a CSV format and can be viewed using any text-based editor. To view the file in a text editor, open the file titled “ARCHIVE.CSV”. All pressures recorded in the file are displayed in inWC units. Use only the USB Flash Drive supplied with the unit for this function.

The following outlines instructions on how to replace consumable components. Contact the manufacturer for replacement information. There are no user serviceable parts internal to this unit. Maintenance consists only of Zero Calibration as recommended and replacement of external filters. The inline filters should be used at all times while the pressure monitor is in operation. This will prevent any dust or moisture from damaging the internal sensors. Using a filter not specified by the manufacturer could result in delayed or incorrect differential pressure readings. Filter replacement is recommended to be done annually for very dirty environments. In normal, clean indoor environments, the filters can be used for up to 5 years.

Answer:
Before connecting any components or applying power, plan your sensor layout. Each sensor must be assigned a room (1 or 2) and a sensor number (1–2). Set these assignments using the DIP switches on each sensor: *Note: Room 1, Sensor 1 is reserved for the built-in pressure sensor and cannot be assigned to an external sensor. Use standard straight-through Ethernet cables (Cat5e or higher). Maximum total cable distance from the RPM to the last sensor should not exceed 50 feet. Daisy-chain additional sensors by connecting an Ethernet cable from one sensor to the next. Do not enable any sensor that is not physically connected, or the system will generate a communication error alarm. Try power cycling the affected sensors by unplugging and re-plugging their Ethernet cables. Some setups may require daisy-chaining left RJ45 to left RJ45 for proper detection.

Answer:
The pressure monitor settings may be accessed using the menu system. The menu system is comprised of a main menu screen and several sub screens. The main menu (shown on the right) is accessed by pushing the settings (gear) icon in the upper right corner of the main screen. The title of the menu is shown at the top. There are five submenu buttons in this menu screen which can be pushed to access the respective submenu. The BACK button is used for navigating to the previous menu. In an alarm condition, the STOP ALARM button will appear at the bottom of the main menu screen. Pressing the STOP ALARM button will silence the audible alarm as well as any external alarms through the web or mobile app if connected. The main screen will remain red until the differential pressure is within the user specified limit. The EXIT button is used for returning to the main screen. A detailed description of the menu structure and the available settings are outlined later in this document. The Room Settings menu will allow you to change various settings for the monitored room.

Answer:
The operator will be prompted to plug in a USB flash drive for downloading. Offloading the historical information from the PPM4 does not destroy any information stored internally. The download process will start and the operator will be returned to the previous menu when the download is complete. Do not remove the USB device until the download is complete. The history is downloaded in a CSV format and can be viewed using any text-based editor. To view the file in a text editor, open the file titled “ARCHIVE.CSV”. All pressures recorded in the file are displayed in inWC units. Use only the USB Flash Drive supplied with the unit for this function. This option allows the operator to erase all of the previously archived messages in the history. A new entry will be added to the archive history stating that the history has been erased. Allows user to set timer for screen going into rest mode, auto lock settings to set timer for locking the device, adjust brightness of screen, change the size of the text displaying pressure, as well as changing the language used in the software.

- How to rpm manual reference chunk 12: there are no user serviceable parts internal to this?
- What do I need to know about rpm manual reference chunk 12: there are no user serviceable parts internal to this?
- Help with rpm manual reference chunk 12: there are no user serviceable parts internal to this?
- RPM RPM Manual reference chunk 12: There are no user serviceable parts internal to this?
Answer:
There are no user serviceable parts internal to this unit. Maintenance consists only of Zero Calibration as recommended and replacement of external filters. The inline filters should be used at all times while the pressure monitor is in operation. This will prevent any dust or moisture from damaging the internal sensors. Using a filter not specified by the manufacturer could result in delayed or incorrect differential pressure readings. Filter replacement is recommended to be done annually for very dirty environments. In normal, clean indoor environments, the filters can be used for up to 5 years. To clean the TFT display use a soft, damp cloth to gently wipe away any dirt or oil. It is best to do this with the system turned off so that dirt and oil is easily identified on the black screen. Use a horizontal or vertical motion to wipe the display. After using the damp cloth, use a dry cloth to wipe away any traces of moisture that may be left on the display. Do not use chemical cleaners on the display. Such actions may cause permanent damage to the display.

- Help with temperature & humidity sensor quick guide reference chunk 1: readings display on the main screen once enabled and?
- Temp/RH Sensor Temperature & Humidity Sensor Quick Guide reference chunk 1: Readings display on the main screen once enabled and?
Answer:
Readings display on the main screen once enabled and can be used for alarms and cloud reporting (if configured). Do NOT connect RPM/PPM4 sensors to a standard network switch/router (this is a sensor bus, not Ethernet networking). Plan which Room (1 or 2) and Sensor number you want this sensor to be assigned to. *Note: Room 1, Sensor 1 is reserved for the built-in pressure sensor and cannot be used by an external sensor. Recommended: Use Room 1, Sensor 2 (DIP4 OFF, DIP1 ON) unless you have a reason to choose otherwise. Connect an Ethernet cable from either RPM/PPM4 RJ45 port to either RJ45 port on the sensor. If you have multiple sensors, daisy-chain them sensor-to-sensor with Ethernet cables. From the main screen go to: Menu → Room Settings → Room 1 → Sensor Settings → Sensor Enable Tip: Do not enable a sensor that isn’t physically connected or you may get a communication error alarm. Important: Alarms only trigger if Room Status = Occupied Check: Menu → Room Settings → Room X → Room Status → Occupied

Questions:
- What does the Temperature & Humidity Sensor Quick Guide say about Some RPM/PPM4 peripheral sensors can receive power from the?
- Find Temp/RH Sensor information about Some RPM/PPM4 peripheral sensors can receive power from the?

- Temperature & Humidity Sensor Quick Guide reference chunk 2: Some RPM/PPM4 peripheral sensors can receive power from the?
- How do I temperature & humidity sensor quick guide reference chunk 2: some rpm/ppm4 peripheral sensors can receive power from the?
- How to temperature & humidity sensor quick guide reference chunk 2: some rpm/ppm4 peripheral sensors can receive power from the?
- What do I need to know about temperature & humidity sensor quick guide reference chunk 2: some rpm/ppm4 peripheral sensors can receive power from the?
- Help with temperature & humidity sensor quick guide reference chunk 2: some rpm/ppm4 peripheral sensors can receive power from the?
- Temp/RH Sensor Temperature & Humidity Sensor Quick Guide reference chunk 2: Some RPM/PPM4 peripheral sensors can receive power from the?
Answer:
Some RPM/PPM4 peripheral sensors can receive power from the RPM/PPM4 expansion bus. However, the expansion bus has a maximum available capacity of 200 mA total for connected peripheral modules. The Cellular Module requires its own power supply because it exceeds the RPM/PPM4 expansion bus capacity. For best performance, Abatement Technologies recommends using the provided power supply for higher-current peripheral sensors, including: Temp/RH and external pressure sensors may be powered by the RPM/PPM4 expansion bus as long as the total connected peripheral load does not exceed 200 mA. Do not add an air velocity sensor, particle sensor, or cellular module to this setup without using the appropriate external power supply. Important: The built-in pressure sensor is internal to the RPM/PPM4 and does not count as an external sensor module on the expansion bus. To receive notifications of software and product updates you must register using this QR code.

Product: Pressure Sensor

Questions:
- What does the Pressure Sensor Quick Guide say about • Install the pressure sensor in the area or?
- Find Pressure Sensor information about • Install the pressure sensor in the area or?

- Where in the docs is • Install the pressure sensor in the area or?
- Pressure Sensor Quick Guide reference chunk 2: • Install the pressure sensor in the area or?
- How do I pressure sensor quick guide reference chunk 2: • install the pressure sensor in the area or?
- How to pressure sensor quick guide reference chunk 2: • install the pressure sensor in the area or?
- What do I need to know about pressure sensor quick guide reference chunk 2: • install the pressure sensor in the area or?
- Help with pressure sensor quick guide reference chunk 2: • install the pressure sensor in the area or?
- Pressure Sensor Pressure Sensor Quick Guide reference chunk 2: • Install the pressure sensor in the area or?
Answer:
• Install the pressure sensor in the area or room you want to monitor. • Mount the sensor where it is protected from damage and easy to access for service. • Avoid placing it: o Where tubing can be pinched, blocked, or disconnected o Directly in front of supply vents or strong airflow o In wet areas or where water spray may contact the sensor o In locations where vibration or impact may affect the sensor If the pressure sensor uses pressure ports or tubing, make sure the tubing is securely connected and not kinked. Confirm the reference side and room side are connected according to your pressure monitoring setup. Confirm the tubing is connected to the correct port for the pressure direction you want to measure. Tip: If the pressure value appears reversed, check the tubing connections and confirm the room/reference ports are not swapped. From the main screen go to: Menu → Room Settings → Room 1 → Sensor Settings → Sensor Enable Tip: Do not enable a sensor that isn’t physically connected or you may get a communication error alarm.

Answer:
The modem uses a standard 3FF (micro-SIM) form factor. In practice, most Canadian and American SIM cards from major carriers will work, as long as the plan is provisioned for LTE-M (Cat-M1) data and the carrier supports the modem’s bands in your area. What works best: IoT/M2M data plans from major carriers in Canada and the U.S. (Rogers, Bell, Telus; Verizon, AT&T, T-Mobile) that explicitly include LTE-M. APN: You’ll need the carrier’s APN. Insert SIM → power on → set APN via AT commands, then attach. Roaming: Cross-border roaming is possible if your plan allows LTE-M roaming; otherwise, use a local SIM. eUICC/eSIM: Supported if delivered as a 3FF profile/SIM by your carrier and provisioned for LTE-M. Locked/Special SIMs: Consumer phone SIMs sometimes block IoT use or LTE-M; if attach fails, ask your carrier to enable LTE-M on the line. Once you have an activated SIM, you will need to ensure it is the correct size of MICRO or 15mm x 12mm. If your SIM is too small, adapters are available for purchase. If the sim is too large, the is usually a casing which can be removed to make it MICRO.

Product: Cellular Module
Category: Document Reference
Source: Cellular Module Setup Quick Guide | https://docs.google.com/document/d/1tCA0fPUpyCAxweMY27g-f1R6prsBPAXw/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=106548179376433005372&rtpof=true&sd=true
Keywords: antennas, application, attaches, bridge, building, calculates, cat-m1, cellular, chunk, cloud, combines, commands, custom, data, device, document, embedded, enabled, enclosure, establishes, fixes, from, gois, gps/gnss, guide, modem, module, monarch, pre-certified, quick, reference, setup, the
Questions:
- What does the Cellular Module Setup Quick Guide say about The Monarch Gois a pre-certified cellular + GPS/GNSS modem?
- Find Cellular Module information about The Monarch Gois a pre-certified cellular + GPS/GNSS modem?
- Search Cellular Module Setup Quick Guide for The Monarch Gois a pre-certified cellular + GPS/GNSS modem?
- I need help with Cellular Module: The Monarch Gois a pre-certified cellular + GPS/GNSS modem?
- Where in the docs is The Monarch Gois a pre-certified cellular + GPS/GNSS modem?
- Cellular Module Setup Quick Guide reference chunk 3: The Monarch Gois a pre-certified cellular + GPS/GNSS modem?
- How do I cellular module setup quick guide reference chunk 3: the monarch gois a pre-certified cellular + gps/gnss modem?
- How to cellular module setup quick guide reference chunk 3: the monarch gois a pre-certified cellular + gps/gnss modem?
- What do I need to know about cellular module setup quick guide reference chunk 3: the monarch gois a pre-certified cellular + gps/gnss modem?
- Help with cellular module setup quick guide reference chunk 3: the monarch gois a pre-certified cellular + gps/gnss modem?
- Cellular Module Cellular Module Setup Quick Guide reference chunk 3: The Monarch Gois a pre-certified cellular + GPS/GNSS modem?
Answer:
The Monarch Gois a pre-certified cellular + GPS/GNSS modem packaged for quick prototyping and integration. It combines an LTE Cat-M1 radio (for low-power, wide-area data on Verizon’s network) with an integrated GNSS receiver and embedded antennas inside a small enclosure. The kit presents the modem over USB as a virtual COM port, so you can send standard AT commands from a terminal or your application without building a custom radio front end. Cellular link (LTE-M): Your device talks to the modem over UART (via the kit’s USB bridge) using AT commands. The modem attaches to the LTE-M network, establishes a data session, and moves IP packets (TCP/UDP/HTTP/MQTT, etc.) to your cloud or server. GNSS positioning: When enabled, the onboard GNSS calculates position fixes (e.g., GPS/Galileo). Your application reads NMEA/AT outputs for coordinates, speed, and time. Power management: The modem supports low-power modes (eDRX/PSM). You enable these via AT commands to trade off latency for battery life. A hardware RING/Wake line and URCs can wake your host when events occur.

- What does the Cellular Module Setup Quick Guide say about Integrated antennas: LTE and GNSS antennas are built in.?

- Help with cellular module setup quick guide reference chunk 4: integrated antennas: lte and gnss antennas are built in.?
- Cellular Module Cellular Module Setup Quick Guide reference chunk 4: Integrated antennas: LTE and GNSS antennas are built in.?
Answer:
Integrated antennas: LTE and GNSS antennas are built in. Follow keep-out guidance (avoid metal directly above the enclosure) for reliable RF performance. Network & bands: LTE-M only (no 2G/3G/Cat-1). Primarily for Verizon bands in North America; roaming/other carriers may not be supported. Throughput & latency: Designed for telemetry, not streaming. Expect hundreds of kbps with variable latency—unsuitable for high-bandwidth or real-time audio/video. GNSS constraints: GNSS needs sky view; performance degrades indoors or under dense cover. Antenna placement: Because antennas are internal, enclosure materials and nearby metal strongly affect performance—respect placement guidelines. In short, this kit is a ready-to-use pathway to add low-power cellular connectivity and location to your device, ideal for sensors, asset trackers, and controllers that send small, periodic messages and can tolerate LTE-M latency characteristics.

- What does the Cellular Module Setup Quick Guide say about Some RPM/PPM4 peripheral sensors can receive power from the?

- Cellular Module Setup Quick Guide reference chunk 5: Some RPM/PPM4 peripheral sensors can receive power from the?
- How do I cellular module setup quick guide reference chunk 5: some rpm/ppm4 peripheral sensors can receive power from the?
- How to cellular module setup quick guide reference chunk 5: some rpm/ppm4 peripheral sensors can receive power from the?
- What do I need to know about cellular module setup quick guide reference chunk 5: some rpm/ppm4 peripheral sensors can receive power from the?
- Help with cellular module setup quick guide reference chunk 5: some rpm/ppm4 peripheral sensors can receive power from the?
- Cellular Module Cellular Module Setup Quick Guide reference chunk 5: Some RPM/PPM4 peripheral sensors can receive power from the?
Answer:
Some RPM/PPM4 peripheral sensors can receive power from the RPM/PPM4 expansion bus. However, the expansion bus has a maximum available capacity of 200 mA total for connected peripheral modules. The Cellular Module requires its own power supply because it exceeds the RPM/PPM4 expansion bus capacity. For best performance, Abatement Technologies recommends using the provided power supply for higher-current peripheral sensors, including: Temp/RH and external pressure sensors may be powered by the RPM/PPM4 expansion bus as long as the total connected peripheral load does not exceed 200 mA. Do not add an air velocity sensor, particle sensor, or cellular module to this setup without using the appropriate external power supply. Important: The built-in pressure sensor is internal to the RPM/PPM4 and does not count as an external sensor module on the expansion bus. To receive notifications of software and product updates you must register using this QR code.

It combines an LTE Cat-M1 radio (for low-power, wide-area data on Verizon’s network) with an integrated GNSS receiver and embedded antennas inside a small enclosure. The kit presents the modem over USB as a virtual COM port, so you can send standard AT commands from a terminal or your application without building a custom radio front end. Cellular link (LTE-M): Your device talks to the modem over UART (via the kit’s USB bridge) using AT commands. The modem attaches to the LTE-M network, establishes a data session, and moves IP packets (TCP/UDP/HTTP/MQTT, etc.) to your cloud or server.

Answer:
GNSS positioning: When enabled, the onboard GNSS calculates position fixes (e.g., GPS/Galileo). Your application reads NMEA/AT outputs for coordinates, speed, and time. Power management: The modem supports low-power modes (eDRX/PSM). You enable these via AT commands to trade off latency for battery life. A hardware RING/Wake line and URCs can wake your host when events occur. Integrated antennas: LTE and GNSS antennas are built in. Follow keep-out guidance (avoid metal directly above the enclosure) for reliable RF performance. Network & bands: LTE-M only (no 2G/3G/Cat-1). Primarily for Verizon bands in North America; roaming/other carriers may not be supported. Throughput & latency: Designed for telemetry, not streaming. Expect hundreds of kbps with variable latency—unsuitable for high-bandwidth or real-time audio/video. Power & I/O levels: I/O is 1.8 V logic at the module. If you later embed the modem, use level shifting for 3.3 V/5 V hosts and provide adequate bulk capacitance for TX current bursts. GNSS constraints: GNSS needs sky view; performance degrades indoors or under dense cover.

Firstly, ensure the device is connected to WIFI or cellular, this can be done through the instructions in the respective menus. in the menu, input the login credentials, these are the same as the credentials used to sign into the web application. If credentials are correct, the system will give an acceptance message After approx. 2 minutes, the device will be moved to the Verified Devices section on the web application Then we will make the PPM4 alarm, turning it onto occupied and setting the alarm points to force an alarm. You can tell it is alarming by the red border and buzzer sounding. Once an alarm is sent from PPM4, we can see a new alarm definition made in the Web Application, we can then go head and click Add Notification Then we will make the PPM4 alarm, turning it onto occupied and setting the alarm points to force an alarm. You can tell it is alarming by the red border and buzzer sounding. Once an alarm is sent from PPM4, we can see a new alarm definition made in the Web Application, we can then go head and click Add Notification

ID: ppm4-wi-fi-document-reference-ppm4-wi-fi-troubleshooting-reference-chu
Title: PPM4 Wi-Fi Troubleshooting reference chunk 1: 1. Device Connects but Fails to Validate This issue
Product: PPM4 Wi-Fi
Category: Document Reference
Source: PPM4 Wi-Fi Troubleshooting | https://docs.google.com/document/d/1WZFT6sMLPnqtEJO-vOJjuNciZxRpv15q/edit?usp=drive_link&ouid=106548179376433005372&rtpof=true&sd=true
Keywords: advanced, appear, arises, available, but, button, chunk, connect, connecting, connects, corporate, cycle, desired, device, displaying, document, does, down, enter, environments, fails, follow, following, issue, ppm4, reference, this, troubleshooting, validate, wi-fi
Questions:
- What does the PPM4 Wi-Fi Troubleshooting say about 1. Device Connects but Fails to Validate This issue?
- Find PPM4 Wi-Fi information about 1. Device Connects but Fails to Validate This issue?
- Search PPM4 Wi-Fi Troubleshooting for 1. Device Connects but Fails to Validate This issue?
- I need help with PPM4 Wi-Fi: 1. Device Connects but Fails to Validate This issue?
- Where in the docs is 1. Device Connects but Fails to Validate This issue?
- PPM4 Wi-Fi Troubleshooting reference chunk 1: 1. Device Connects but Fails to Validate This issue?
- How do I ppm4 wi-fi troubleshooting reference chunk 1: 1. device connects but fails to validate this issue?
- How to ppm4 wi-fi troubleshooting reference chunk 1: 1. device connects but fails to validate this issue?
- What do I need to know about ppm4 wi-fi troubleshooting reference chunk 1: 1. device connects but fails to validate this issue?
- Help with ppm4 wi-fi troubleshooting reference chunk 1: 1. device connects but fails to validate this issue?
- PPM4 Wi-Fi PPM4 Wi-Fi Troubleshooting reference chunk 1: 1. Device Connects but Fails to Validate This issue?
Answer:
1. Device Connects but Fails to Validate This issue typically arises when connecting to networks with advanced security measures, such as those found in hospitals or corporate environments. If the device successfully connects but does not validate, try the following steps: Once the device successfully validates on the hotspot, reconnect to the original network. 2. Wi-Fi Network Not Displaying If the desired Wi-Fi network does not appear in the available network list, follow these steps: - Power cycle the PPM4 device by holding down the power button for 10 seconds. This can help refresh the network scan. - If the network is still not visible, select the "HIDDEN SSID" option and manually enter your network name (SSID) to connect. 3. Device Still Will Not Connect If the device continues to experience connectivity issues, it may be necessary to involve your network’s IT team. Specifically, they may need to "greenlist" the PPM4 device, ensuring it is recognized and allowed on the network. Consider the following when diagnosing issues.

ID: ppm4-troubleshooting-ppm4-screen-is-alarming
Title: PPM4 screen is alarming
Product: PPM4
Category: Troubleshooting
Source: PPM4 | 
Keywords: alarming, fix, issue, ppm4, problem, screen, support, troubleshoot, troubleshooting
Questions:
- Why is my PPM4 not working?
- PPM4 troubleshooting?
- PPM4 screen is alarming?
- What do I check if ppm4 screen is alarming?
- fix ppm4 screen is alarming?
- How do I ppm4 screen is alarming?
- How to ppm4 screen is alarming?
- What do I need to know about ppm4 screen is alarming?
- Help with ppm4 screen is alarming?
- PPM4 PPM4 screen is alarming?
Answer:
If the PPM4 is alarming, review the room/pressure status, use STOP ALARM only to silence the audible alert, and correct the condition that caused pressure to move outside limits. The screen may remain red until values return within limits.

ID: ppm4-troubleshooting-ppm4-cannot-download-log
Title: PPM4 cannot download log
Product: PPM4
Category: Troubleshooting
Source: PPM4 | 
Keywords: cannot, download, fix, issue, log, ppm4, problem, support, troubleshoot, troubleshooting
Questions:
- Why is my PPM4 not working?
- PPM4 troubleshooting?
- PPM4 cannot download log?
- What do I check if ppm4 cannot download log?
- fix ppm4 cannot download log?
- How do I ppm4 cannot download log?
- How to ppm4 cannot download log?
- What do I need to know about ppm4 cannot download log?
- Help with ppm4 cannot download log?
- PPM4 PPM4 cannot download log?
Answer:
For PPM4 log downloads, use the supplied USB drive if applicable, wait until the download completes, and do not remove the drive early. If data does not export, retry with the correct USB media and check the Data Log menu.

ID: ppm4-troubleshooting-ppm4-wrong-pressure-readings
Title: PPM4 wrong pressure readings
Product: PPM4
Category: Troubleshooting
Source: PPM4 | 
Keywords: fix, issue, ppm4, pressure, problem, readings, support, troubleshoot, troubleshooting, wrong
Questions:
- Why is my PPM4 not working?
- PPM4 troubleshooting?
- PPM4 wrong pressure readings?
- What do I check if ppm4 wrong pressure readings?
- fix ppm4 wrong pressure readings?
- How do I ppm4 wrong pressure readings?
- How to ppm4 wrong pressure readings?
- What do I need to know about ppm4 wrong pressure readings?
- Help with ppm4 wrong pressure readings?
- PPM4 PPM4 wrong pressure readings?
Answer:
If PPM4 pressure readings look wrong, check Room and Reference tubing, verify filters and tube orientation, confirm no tubes are pinched/blocked, avoid blowing into ports, and calibrate only according to procedure.

ID: ppm4-troubleshooting-ppm4-cannot-connect-to-cloud
Title: PPM4 cannot connect to cloud
Product: PPM4
Category: Troubleshooting
Source: PPM4 |

- How to ppm4 cannot connect to cloud?
- What do I need to know about ppm4 cannot connect to cloud?
- Help with ppm4 cannot connect to cloud?
- PPM4 PPM4 cannot connect to cloud?
Answer:
If PPM4 will not connect to cloud, verify Wi-Fi/cellular connection, device registration, credentials entered in Cloud Setup, and whether the network allows validation. Try a hotspot to rule out corporate network restrictions.

ID: ppm4-troubleshooting-ppm4-sensor-values-missing
Title: PPM4 sensor values missing
Product: PPM4
Category: Troubleshooting
Source: PPM4 | 
Keywords: fix, issue, missing, ppm4, problem, sensor, support, troubleshoot, troubleshooting, values
Questions:
- Why is my PPM4 not working?
- PPM4 troubleshooting?
- PPM4 sensor values missing?
- What do I check if ppm4 sensor values missing?
- fix ppm4 sensor values missing?
- How do I ppm4 sensor values missing?
- How to ppm4 sensor values missing?
- What do I need to know about ppm4 sensor values missing?
- Help with ppm4 sensor values missing?
- PPM4 PPM4 sensor values missing?
Answer:
If PPM4 sensor values do not appear, verify DIP switches, sensor enable settings, power, Ethernet cables, total cable distance, and consistent daisy-chain wiring. Do not use a standard network switch/router.

ID: at-connect-mobile-app-overview
Title: AT Connect mobile app overview

ID: scrubber-selector-how-it-works-v31
Title: How the Scrubber Selector chooses a scrubber
Product: Scrubber Selector
Category: Scrubber Sizing
Source: Scrubber Selector | /scrubber-selector
Keywords: scrubber selector, how it works, recommendation logic, location, project type, hazard, goal, room size, ach, cfm, pressure monitor
Questions:
- How does the scrubber selector work?
- How does MonSuite pick a scrubber?
- What information does the scrubber selector use?
- Why did it recommend this scrubber?
- How does AbateBot choose scrubbers?
Answer:
The Scrubber Selector asks for location, project type, hazard or concern, primary goal, room size, setup details, power, and site/cabinet preference. It then chooses a rule set, calculates room volume, target ACH, design CFM, ducting derating, effective scrubber CFM, quantity, achieved ACH, electrical load, and whether pressure monitoring/documentation should be recommended. The output is an estimating recommendation, not a compliance stamp.

ID: scrubber-sizing-ach-cfm-calculation
Title: Scrubber sizing: ACH and CFM calculation
Product: Scrubber Selector
Category: Scrubber Sizing
Source: Pressure Guide | /pressure-monitoring
Keywords: ach, cfm, room volume, sizing, scrubber quantity, air changes per hour, calculate scrubber, design ach
Questions:
- How many scrubbers do I need?
- How do I size a scrubber?
- How do I calculate ACH?
- How do I calculate CFM needed?
- What is the scrubber sizing formula?
- How much airflow do I need?
Answer:
Scrubber sizing starts with room volume: length × width × height. Required CFM is calculated as ACH × room volume ÷ 60. MonSuite then applies a safety factor and ducting/filter derating, divides the design CFM by effective scrubber CFM, and rounds up to the next whole unit. Always round up: 1.2 units means 2 units.

ID: scrubber-pressure-monitor-recommendation
Title: When a scrubber also needs a pressure monitor
Product: Scrubber Selector
Category: Pressure Monitoring
Source: Pressure Guide | /pressure-monitoring
Keywords: pressure monitor, negative pressure, positive pressure, containment, prove pressure, ppm4, rpm, air scrubber, monitor required, alarm logging
Questions:
- Do I need a pressure monitor with a scrubber?
- Does a scrubber prove negative pressure?
- When should I recommend RPM or PPM4 with a scrubber?
- Can the scrubber prove containment?
- Do negative air machines need pressure monitoring?
Answer:
A scrubber or negative air machine creates airflow, but it does not prove the room is holding the required pressure relationship. If the goal is negative pressure, positive pressure, healthcare isolation, healthcare construction containment, asbestos containment, or project documentation, recommend a calibrated pressure monitor such as RPM or PPM4. The monitor provides the live pressure reading, alarm, and record that the space stayed under control.

ID: scrubber-h2km-vs-bd2k-cabinet-choice
Title: H2KM vs BD2K: metal vs plastic cabinet choice
Product: Scrubbers
Category: Product Selection
Source: Scrubber Selector | /scrubber-selector
Keywords: h2km, h2kma, bd2k, bulldog, metal, plastic, cabinet, residential, wet area, finished space, scratch, 2,000 cfm, same class
Questions:
- Should I choose H2KM or BD2K?
- What is the difference between H2KM and BD2K?
- Which scrubber is better for residential?
- Which scrubber is better for wet areas?
- Is BD2K the plastic version of H2KM?
- When should I use the metal scrubber?
Answer:
H2KM/H2KMA and BD2K-XHP/BD2K-XHPA are treated as the same 2,000 CFM class in MonSuite. Use BD2K when the plastic cabinet is better: residential work, finished spaces where metal could scratch surfaces, or wet/damp restoration areas. Use H2KM/H2KMA as the metal-cabinet workhorse everywhere else, especially general commercial, healthcare, industrial, and abatement work.

ID: scrubber-real-main-lineup-summary
Title: Real scrubber lineup in MonSuite
Product: Scrubbers
Category: Product Selection
Source: Scrubber Selector | /scrubber-selector
Keywords: real scrubbers, main lineup, hc800, pred750, h2km, bd2k, pas2400, pas5000, scrubber list, abatement scrubbers
Questions:
- What real scrubbers are in MonSuite?
- What scrubbers can the selector recommend?
- What are the main Abatement scrubbers?
- List the real scrubbers in the selector.
Answer:
MonSuite’s real scrubber lineup includes HC800FD/HC800FDUV, PREDATOR PRED750, H2KM/H2KMA, BD2K-XHP/BD2K-XHPA, PAS2400, and PAS5000. The selector uses PRED750 for smaller portable jobs, H2KM or BD2K for the 2,000 CFM class, PAS2400 when high 120V airflow and mobility/stairs matter, and PAS5000 for major large-containment jobs with confirmed 230V/30A power.

ID: scrubber-pred750-when-to-choose
Title: When to choose PRED750
Product: PRED750
Category: Product Selection
Source: PRED750 Manual | /scrubber-selector
Keywords: pred750, predator, 750 cfm, portable, small room, lightweight, 10 inch duct, carbon, vapor lock, residential
Questions:
- When should I use PRED750?
- Is PRED750 good for small rooms?
- What is PRED750 airflow?
- What filters does PRED750 use?
- Is PRED750 portable?
Answer:
Choose PRED750 when the job needs a smaller portable scrubber around the 750 CFM class. It is lighter than the large units, uses 120V/15A power, supports negative pressure, recirculation, and positive-pressure support, and has 10-inch nominal inlet/outlet duct connections. It is a good fit for smaller rooms, localized containment, and jobs where a 2,000+ CFM unit is too large.

ID: scrubber-pas2400-when-to-choose-v31
Title: When to choose PAS2400
Product: PAS2400
Category: Product Selection
Source: PAS2400 Manual | /scrubber-selector
Keywords: pas2400, 2100 cfm, stair climber, 120v, high airflow, stainless, mobility, stairs, h1980p, 12 inch duct
Questions:
- When should I choose PAS2400?
- Is PAS2400 good for stairs?
- What is PAS2400 airflow?
- Does PAS2400 run on 120V?
- Should I choose PAS2400 or H2KM?
Answer:
Choose PAS2400 when the job needs high airflow on 120V and mobility matters. It is a 2,100 CFM high-speed unit with a transport dolly/stair-climber style setup, stainless cabinet, 120V/15A circuit requirement, and 12-inch duct adapter support. It can be a better fit than a box-style 2,000 CFM unit when access, movement, or stairs are important.

ID: scrubber-pas5000-power-warning-large-containment
Title: PAS5000: large-containment scrubber with 230V power requirement
Product: PAS5000
Category: Product Selection
Source: PAS5000 Manual | /scrubber-selector
Keywords: pas5000, 4000 cfm, 230v, 30a, large containment, power warning, big scrubber, high airflow, 14 inch outlet, h2080p
Questions:
- When should I choose PAS5000?
- Why is PAS5000 not showing when I choose 120V?
- What power does PAS5000 need?
- What is the biggest scrubber in the selector?
- Can PAS5000 run on 120V?
Answer:
PAS5000 is the large-containment option in the selector: 4,000 CFM class airflow with 230V/30A power and up to 21A normal operating draw. Do not recommend PAS5000 for a user who only has 120V available. If power is unknown, it can appear with a warning that 230V/30A power must be confirmed before the recommendation is relied on.

ID: scrubber-carbon-voc-filter-selection
Title: Carbon/VOC filter guidance for scrubbers
Product: Scrubbers
Category: Filters
Source: Scrubber Selector | /scrubber-selector
Keywords: carbon, voc, odor, vapor lock, ovg, smell, gas, vl2024, vl2002, vl1002, hepa not odor
Questions:
- Do HEPA filters remove odors?
- Which scrubber should I use for VOC?
- Do I need carbon filters for odor?
- What is VAPOR-LOCK?
- Can a scrubber remove smell?
Answer:
HEPA filters capture particles; they do not remove gases, odors, vapors, or VOCs by themselves. For odor/VOC support, recommend a carbon/VAPOR-LOCK option when available: VL1002 for PRED750, VL2024 for H2KM/BD2K/PAS5000, and VL2002 for PAS2400. Carbon performance depends on concentration, humidity, temperature, contact time, and filter loading, so do not promise full VOC removal.

ID: scrubber-ducting-derating-airflow-loss
Title: Ducting and filter loading reduce scrubber airflow
Product: Scrubbers
Category: Ducting
Source: Scrubber Selector | /scrubber-selector
Keywords: ducting, derating, flex duct, rigid duct, bends, airflow loss, filter loading, reduced voltage, cfm loss
Questions:
- Why does ducting reduce airflow?
- How does MonSuite derate scrubber CFM?
- Does flex duct reduce scrubber performance?
- Why did the selector recommend more units than simple math?
- What derating should I use?
Answer:
Actual installed airflow can be lower than rated airflow because of duct length, bends, flexible duct, filter loading, reduced voltage, blocked inlets/outlets, and site conditions. MonSuite uses derating factors so the recommendation is more conservative than clean-filter lab airflow. Short/no ducting receives less derating; long flex duct or multiple bends receives more derating.

ID: scrubber-120v-vs-230v-power-selection
Title: Scrubber power selection: 120V vs 230V
Product: Scrubbers
Category: Electrical
Source: Scrubber Selector | /scrubber-selector
Keywords: 120v, 230v, 240v, 15a, 30a, power, circuit, amps, pas5000, pas2400, h2km, bd2k, pred750
Questions:
- Which scrubbers can run on 120V?
- Why do I need 230V for PAS5000?
- What scrubber can I use on a 15 amp circuit?
- How does power affect scrubber selection?
Answer:
Power availability is a major selector filter. PRED750, H2KM/H2KMA, BD2K, and PAS2400 are treated as 120V units with 15A circuit planning, though each has its own amp draw. PAS5000 is the large 230V/30A unit and should not be recommended when the user only has 120V. If power is unknown, MonSuite warns the user to confirm circuits before relying on the recommendation.

ID: scrubber-selector-product-lineup-v33
Title: Real scrubber lineup and when to use each one
Product: Scrubbers
Category: Product Selection
Source: MonSuite V33 Scrubber Selector | /scrubber-selector
Keywords: PRED750, HC800FD, BD2K, H2KM, PAS2400, PAS5000, which scrubber, scrubber lineup, sales fit
Questions:
- Which scrubber should I sell?
- When do I use PRED750?
- When do I use HC800FD?
- When do I use BD2K instead of H2KM?
- When do I use PAS2400?
- When do I use PAS5000?
Answer:
Use PRED750 for smaller rooms, residential work, and compact containment. Use HC800FD/HC800FDUV for healthcare-style occupied spaces, temporary isolation support, and cleaner HEPA air purification applications. Use BD2K for residential, finished-space, wet/damp, or scratch-sensitive 2,000 CFM class jobs. Use H2KM/H2KMA as the metal-cabinet 2,000 CFM workhorse for commercial, industrial, healthcare construction, and abatement. Use PAS2400 for high-CFM 120V jobs where mobility or stairs matter. Use PAS5000 for very large containments only when 230V/30A power and site access are confirmed.

ID: scrubber-selector-pressure-monitor-v33
Title: When the scrubber selector recommends a pressure monitor
Product: Scrubber Selector
Category: Pressure Monitoring
Source: MonSuite V33 Scrubber Selector | /scrubber-selector
Keywords: pressure monitor, scrubber, negative pressure, positive pressure, containment, need monitor, quote monitor
Questions:
- Do I need a pressure monitor with a scrubber?
- When should I quote a pressure monitor?
- Does a scrubber prove negative pressure?
- Can the selector tell me if pressure monitoring is needed?
Answer:
Quote or strongly recommend a pressure monitor whenever the project involves airborne isolation, healthcare construction containment, positive-pressure clean-space protection, asbestos containment, or any goal that includes negative or positive pressure. Scrubbers create airflow; the pressure monitor proves whether the room or containment is actually holding the intended pressure relationship. For simple recirculating air cleaning only, the monitor is usually optional unless documentation is requested.

ID: airflow-planner-room-volume-v37
Title: Room volume and layout interaction
Product: MonSuite
Category: Scrubbers
Source: MonSuite Airflow Planner | /scrubber-selector
Keywords: volume, room size, draw box, walls, resize, length, width, height, calculate room
Questions:
- How does MonSuite calculate room volume?
- Can I enter room volume directly?
- Can I draw walls to set the room size?
Answer:
MonSuite calculates room volume from length × width × height. In the Airflow Planner, changing the dimensions resizes the room box. Users can also enter a known cubic-foot volume and press Draw box; MonSuite estimates a room footprint using the current height and shape ratio. Dragging the right or bottom room handles updates length and width, which recalculates volume and scrubber sizing live.

ID: airflow-planner-duct-types-v37
Title: Duct options in the airflow planner
Product: MonSuite
Category: Scrubbers
Source: MonSuite Airflow Planner | /scrubber-selector
Keywords: duct, ducting, round duct, square duct, flexible duct, flex duct, derating, airflow loss
Questions:
- What duct types are in the planner?
- Does flexible duct affect the recommendation?
- Why does ducting reduce airflow?
Answer:
The Airflow Planner includes round, square, and flexible duct objects. Duct type, duct length, bends, and filter loading affect the airflow derating used in the scrubber calculation. Flexible duct and long/bent runs are treated more conservatively because they usually create more static resistance and reduce installed airflow.

ID: airflow-planner-site-filtering-v37
Title: Site conditions filter available scrubbers
Product: MonSuite
Category: Scrubbers
Source: MonSuite Airflow Planner | /scrubber-selector
Keywords: wet area, residential, finished space, hide units, BD2K, H2KM, plastic, metal, dry units
Questions:
- Why are some scrubbers hidden?
- Why does wet area show BD2K?
- Should I use H2KM or BD2K?
Answer:
The Airflow Planner filters draggable scrubbers based on site condition. For wet/damp, residential, finished, or scratch-sensitive spaces, it prioritizes plastic-cabinet units like BD2K and compact options like PRED750. For dry commercial, industrial, healthcare construction, abatement, and general jobsite work, it allows the metal workhorse units such as H2KM, PAS2400, and PAS5000 when appropriate.
